# Architecture Decision Records

One file per decision, named `ADR-NNNN-short-slug.md`. Numbers are assigned in
order and never reused.

An ADR is immutable once **Accepted**. A decision that changes does not get edited
— a new ADR supersedes it, and the old one is marked `Superseded by ADR-NNNN`.
The value of the record is that it shows what was believed *at the time*.

Write one when a choice is expensive to reverse, when a reasonable person would
have chosen differently, or when the reasoning would otherwise be lost. Routine
choices do not need one.

| [0018](ADR-0018-the-mingw-curl-8-21-0-build-is-the-windows-http-reference.md) | The mingw build of curl 8.21.0 is the Windows reference for HTTP; Curl may differ from System32 `curl.exe` only where that build lacks a feature | Accepted | 2026-09-26 |
| [0019](ADR-0019-numeric-option-ceiling-matches-the-platform-curl.md) | The ceiling of a numeric option matches the platform curl: 2^31-1 on Windows, 2^63-1 on Linux and macOS | Accepted | 2026-09-26 |
| [0020](ADR-0020-compressed-advertises-deflate-gzip-and-br-until-a-zstd-decoder-exists.md) | `--compressed` advertises `deflate, gzip, br` until a zstd decoder exists | Superseded by ADR-0287 | 2026-09-26 |
| [0021](ADR-0021-v-version-keeps-curls-format-and-lists-only-what-curl-implements.md) | `-V`/`--version` keeps curl's format and version number and lists only what Curl implements, on each platform | Accepted | 2026-09-26 |
| [0022](ADR-0022-basic-and-bearer-credentials-are-sent-as-the-platform-curl-sends-them.md) | Basic and Bearer credentials are sent as the platform curl sends them: the ANSI code page on Windows, UTF-8 elsewhere, pre-emptively only for exactly one scheme | Accepted | 2026-09-26 |
| [0023](ADR-0023-the-connector-tunnels-through-an-http-proxy-as-curl-8-21-0-does.md) | The connector tunnels through an HTTP proxy as curl 8.21.0 does: measured CONNECT bytes, exit 7/56/5 as measured, HTTPS and SOCKS proxies not yet | Accepted | 2026-09-26 |
| [0024](ADR-0024-proxy-selection-follows-the-platform-curl-and-reads-an-injected-environment.md) | Proxy selection follows the platform curl and reads an injected environment | Accepted | 2026-09-26 |
| [0025](ADR-0025-digest-answers-as-curls-own-digest-code-on-every-platform.md) | Digest answers as curl's own Digest code does on every platform, not as Windows' WDigest does for the Schannel build | Accepted | 2026-09-26 |
| [0026](ADR-0026-auth-scheme-and-proxy-options-parse-as-curls-tool-keeps-them.md) | The auth-scheme and proxy options parse as curl's tool keeps them: one scheme bit set, one proxy slot, an unknown proxy scheme fails the transfer with exit 7 | Accepted | 2026-09-26 |
| [0027](ADR-0027-multipart-form-bodies-are-built-as-libcurl-8-21-0-builds-them.md) | Multipart form bodies are built as libcurl 8.21.0 builds them: measured headers and bytes, files streamed, exit 26 before connecting | Accepted | 2026-09-26 |
| [0028](ADR-0028-auth-scheme-ranking-follows-libcurl-with-no-fallback.md) | The auth scheme is ranked as libcurl ranks it - Negotiate, Bearer, Digest, NTLM, Basic - with no fallback from a scheme not built | Accepted | 2026-09-26 |
| [0029](ADR-0029-output-options-pair-with-urls-as-curls-tool-pairs-them.md) | The output options pair with URLs as curl's tool pairs them: one list of URL and output entries, `--remote-name-all` a creation-time default, `-w @file` read as `-d @file` is | Accepted | 2026-09-26 |
| [0030](ADR-0030-connect-timings-are-taken-by-the-connector-with-the-handshake-from-the-tls-provider.md) | Connect timings are taken by the connector, with the handshake's end from the TLS provider; the dialer reports the local end point | Accepted | 2026-09-26 |
| [0031](ADR-0031-corrupt-compressed-bodies-report-curls-generic-exit-61-text-where-zlib-text-is-unavailable.md) | Corrupt `--compressed` bodies report curl's generic exit 61 text where zlib's own text is unavailable | Accepted | 2026-09-26 |
| [0032](ADR-0032-url-globs-expand-as-curl-8-21-0s-tool-expands-them.md) | URL globs expand as curl 8.21.0's tool expands them: measured messages and columns, lazy expansion, `#N` as `glob_match_url` | Accepted | 2026-09-26 |
| [0033](ADR-0033-a-followed-put-redirect-rewinds-a-seekable-upload-and-passes-stdin-on-as-it-is.md) | A followed PUT redirect rewinds a seekable `-T` upload and passes standard input on as it is | Accepted | 2026-09-26 |
| [0034](ADR-0034-http-authentication-retries-a-401-once-as-curl-8-21-0-does.md) | HTTP authentication retries a 401 once, as curl 8.21.0 does | Accepted | 2026-09-26 |
| [0035](ADR-0035-w-times-print-microseconds-since-the-start-and-speeds-divide-by-time-total.md) | `-w` times print microseconds since the start, and speeds divide by `time_total` | Accepted | 2026-09-26 |
| [0036](ADR-0036-http-request-bodies-are-framed-and-counted-as-curl-8-21-0-frames-them.md) | HTTP request bodies are framed and counted as curl 8.21.0 frames them: `--json` headers after every `-H`, a failed read ends a chunked body, an early final status is the response, `RequestSize` includes the body | Accepted | 2026-09-26 |
| [0037](ADR-0037-a-z-value-that-is-not-a-date-is-read-as-a-file-through-idatafilereader.md) | A `-z` value that is not a date is read as a file through `IDataFileReader`, with curl's Windows filetime warning | Accepted | 2026-09-26 |
| [0038](ADR-0038-w-time-follows-the-windows-c-runtime-strftime-and-onerror-reads-transfer-failed.md) | `-w %time{…}` follows the Windows C runtime's `strftime` with en-US names, and `%{onerror}` reads `IWriteOutVariableSource.TransferFailed` | Accepted | 2026-09-26 |
| [0039](ADR-0039-a-64-bit-numeric-option-value-saturates-where-commandlineoptions-holds-less.md) | A 64-bit numeric option value saturates where `CommandLineOptions` holds less: `--tftp-blksize` and `--max-redirs` at 2^31-1, the timeouts at the longest `TimeSpan` | Accepted | 2026-09-26 |
| [0040](ADR-0040-http-enforces-max-time-and-connect-timeout-in-the-handler.md) | The HTTP handler enforces `-m` and `--connect-timeout` itself on the transfer's clock, and maps failed sends to exit 55 | Accepted | 2026-09-26 |
| [0041](ADR-0041-multipart-encoder-parts-are-encoded-whole-while-the-body-is-built.md) | Multipart `;encoder=` parts are encoded whole while the body is built: `binary` and `8bit` files still stream, `base64`, `quoted-printable` and `7bit` are read and encoded in memory, and a `7bit` refusal is reported after every other part | Accepted; file parts superseded by ADR-0076 | 2026-09-26 |
| [0042](ADR-0042-the-sws-emulation-carries-out-timing-commands-on-an-injected-clock-and-leaves-delay-unsupported.md) | The sws emulation carries out `idle`, `stream`, `writedelay`, `connection-monitor`, `upgrade` and `<postcmd>` `wait` on an injected clock, and leaves `delay` unsupported | Accepted | 2026-09-26 |
| [0043](ADR-0043-w-variables-with-a-fixed-value-print-it-and-those-without-a-source-stay-unknown.md) | `-w` variables with a fixed value print it (`ssl_verify_result`, `proxy_ssl_verify_result`, `tls_earlydata`, `num_retries`, `ftp_entry_path`), `time_queue` is the handler's start, and those without a source stay unknown until BL-302 to BL-305 | Accepted; amended 2026-09-29 by BL-664: `num_retries`, `ftp_entry_path` and the unknown groups now have sources, three values stay fixed; amended 2026-10-01 by BL-906: `tls_earlydata` is the early data bytes the TLS provider reports | 2026-09-26 |
| [0044](ADR-0044-http-honours-range-resume-time-condition-and-max-filesize.md) | How the HTTP handler honours `-r`, `-C`, `-z` and `--max-filesize`: header placement, no `Range` with a request body, RFC 9110 dates for `Last-Modified`, an unmet `-z` reported as 304 | Accepted | 2026-09-26 |
| [0045](ADR-0045-the-transfer-context-carries-a-progress-sink-for-the-progress-meter.md) | The transfer context carries a progress sink for the progress meter: `ITransferProgress` on `ITransferContext.Progress`, `NoTransferProgress` by default, no clock in the sink | Accepted | 2026-09-26 |
| [0046](ADR-0046-the-transfer-context-carries-a-transfer-event-sink-for-v-and-trace.md) | The transfer context carries a transfer event sink for `-v` and `--trace`: `ITransferEvents` on `ITransferContext.Events` and `ConnectTarget.Events`, a sibling of the progress sink, `NoTransferEvents` by default | Accepted | 2026-09-26 |
| [0047](ADR-0047-the-sws-emulation-selects-reply-parts-by-authorization-swsbounce-and-connect-for-one-case.md) | The sws emulation selects reply parts by `Authorization:`, `swsbounce` and `CONNECT` as sws does, taking every request to name its one case | Accepted | 2026-09-26 |
| [0048](ADR-0048-o-names-are-sanitized-on-windows-by-a-separate-step-only-when-globbing.md) | `-o` names are sanitized on Windows by a separate step, only when globbing: `WindowsOutputFileNameSanitizer`, run by `UrlGlobMatch.ResolveOutputFileName` when the caller passes `sanitizesForWindows`, never under `-g` | Accepted | 2026-09-27 |
| [0049](ADR-0049-the-public-suffix-list-is-an-embedded-dated-snapshot-refreshed-by-a-script.md) | The Public Suffix List is an embedded, dated snapshot refreshed by a script: `Curl.Cookies.UnitLibrary/PublicSuffixList/public_suffix_list.dat`, parsed by hand, MPL-2.0 notice kept with it, refreshed by `Update-PublicSuffixList.ps1` | Accepted | 2026-09-27 |
| [0050](ADR-0050-connections-are-reused-across-requests-and-urls-through-a-pooling-connector.md) | Connections are reused across requests and URLs through a pooling connector: `IConnection.MarkReusable`, a key of scheme, host, port, TLS and proxy, five idle connections per run, reuse reported on `ConnectResult` for `%{num_connects}` and `-v` | Accepted | 2026-09-27 |
| [0051](ADR-0051-t-urls-are-resolved-before-the-transfer-and-the-t-file-opened-after-its-warnings.md) | `-T` URLs are resolved before the transfer, and the `-T` file opened after its warnings: `-T` values pair with URLs in order, `UploadTransferUrl` appends, parses with `CurlUrl` and normalises, exit 3 before any warning, exit 26 with `cannot open` lines even under `-s` | Accepted | 2026-09-27 |
| [0052](ADR-0052-handler-synthesised-header-lines-are-reported-as-pseudo-headers.md) | Handler-synthesised header lines are reported as pseudo-headers: `TransferReport.PseudoHeaders` counts towards `%{num_headers}` but is never found by `%header{}`; `file://` reports its three | Accepted | 2026-09-27 |
| [0053](ADR-0053-curl-console-refuses-a-proxy-tunnel-the-connector-cannot-open-yet-with-exit-4.md) | Curl.Console refuses a proxy tunnel the connector cannot open yet, with exit 4: SOCKS proxies, and HTTPS proxies for `https` or under `-p` or `-L`, until BL-213 and BL-266 land (BL-328) | Retired by BL-328 | 2026-09-27 |
| [0054](ADR-0054-the-peer-certificate-chain-is-captured-in-the-tls-provider-and-printed-by-a-port-of-curls-certinfo.md) | The peer certificate chain is captured in the TLS provider's validation callback, carried as DER on `ConnectResult.PeerCertificates` and `TransferReport.PeerCertificates`, and printed for `%{certs}` by `PeerCertificateText`, a port of curl's `Curl_extract_certinfo` | Accepted | 2026-09-27 |
| [0055](ADR-0055-t-uploads-over-http-are-sent-as-put-in-curl-s-64-kib-upload-buffer.md) | `-T` uploads over HTTP are sent as PUT, read in curl's 64 KiB upload buffer: `Content-Length` for a file, chunked for stdin, no `Content-Type`, the first read sharing the buffer with the head, exit 26 `client read function EOF fail` | Accepted | 2026-09-27 |
| [0056](ADR-0056-non-http-schemes-tunnel-through-the-selected-proxy-and-ftp-is-forwarded-as-an-http-get.md) | Non-HTTP schemes tunnel through the selected proxy and `ftp://` is forwarded as an HTTP GET: `ITransferContext.Proxy` set for every scheme but `file`, TCP handlers set `ConnectTarget.Proxy` whatever `-p` says, `ftp` without `-p` goes to the HTTP handler, `tftp` fails with exit 7 as the reference build does, the ADR-0053 guard covers every tunnelled scheme | Accepted | 2026-09-27 |
| [0057](ADR-0057-a-t-upload-over-http-resumes-from-c-with-curls-content-range.md) | A `-T` upload over HTTP resumes from `-C` with curl's `Content-Range`: `bytes N-(L-1)/L` after skipping N bytes, exit 18 at or past the end, exit 26 for an empty source, standard input sent whole with `bytes N-(N-2)/(N-1)`, `-C -` left to BL-351 | Accepted | 2026-09-27 |
| [0058](ADR-0058-proxy-used-is-reported-by-the-http-handler-when-the-transfer-is-set-to-go-through-a-proxy.md) | `%{proxy_used}` is `TransferReport.UsedProxy`, set by the HTTP handler whenever a proxy was selected, forwarded or tunnelled, even when connecting to it fails | Accepted | 2026-09-27 |
| [0059](ADR-0059-the-connect-tunnel-takes-a-and-the-platform-credential-encoding-from-the-composition.md) | The CONNECT tunnel sends the `-A` value (none for `-A ""`, `curl/8.21.0` without it) and encodes the proxy credential as the platform curl does; the proxy itself stays on the transfer context, no decorator | Accepted | 2026-09-27 |
| [0060](ADR-0060-referer-filename-effective-conn-id-and-xfer-id-come-from-curl-console.md) | `%{referer}`, `%{filename_effective}`, `%{conn_id}` and `%{xfer_id}` are set by `Curl.Console` on `TransferWriteOutVariables`; `conn_id` is `-1` for a URL rejected with exit 1 or 3 | Accepted | 2026-09-27 |
| [0061](ADR-0061-an-https-proxy-is-tunnelled-over-its-own-tls-handshake-through-the-transfers-tls-provider.md) | An HTTPS proxy gets TLS to the proxy host, then the HTTP-proxy CONNECT inside it, then target TLS inside that; the proxy handshake uses the transfer's TLS provider until the `--proxy-*` TLS options land (BL-362) | Accepted; proxy TLS provider superseded by ADR-0095 | 2026-09-27 |
| [0062](ADR-0062-form-parts-from-standard-input-read-it-once-across-urls.md) | `-F name=@-` and `-F name=<-` read standard input once: the first URL matches curl, a later part or URL sends an empty part with a matching `Content-Length` and exit 0, where curl declares the first length and exits 26 | Accepted | 2026-09-27 |
| [0063](ADR-0063-write-out-json-is-hand-written-from-typed-variable-values.md) | `%{json}` and `%{header_json}` are hand-written from typed variable values: curl's key order, `null` for a missing text value, curl's escaping, and `curl_version` from this tool's own `-V` library text | Accepted | 2026-09-27 |
| [0064](ADR-0064-variable-content-is-bytes-and-expands-through-utf-8-option-values.md) | `--variable` content is bytes, expanded through UTF-8 option values (its U+FFFD clause superseded by ADR-0934); `%name` reads the process environment; a `name@file` open failure is worded as the Windows curl words it | Accepted | 2026-09-27 |
| [0065](ADR-0065-http-progress-reports-only-the-delivered-body-and-never-goes-back-on-a-retry.md) | The HTTP handler reports "started" once the first connection is made, counts only the body the output receives and the body sent, and never reports a count below one already reported, so a retry that resends a body holds the counter | Accepted | 2026-09-27 |
| [0066](ADR-0066-a-schannel-cert-store-path-is-parsed-as-curl-parses-it-and-opened-through-x509store.md) | A Schannel `--cert` store path (`CurrentUser\MY\<thumbprint>`) is parsed as curl's `get_cert_location` parses it and opened through `X509Store` behind `IClientCertificateStore`; `CurrentUser` and `LocalMachine` open, the six other locations report the open failure | Accepted | 2026-09-27 |
| [0067](ADR-0067-command-line-header-text-becomes-bytes-in-the-platform-curls-encoding-inside-the-http-formatter.md) | Command-line text in the request head (`-H`, `--proxy-header`) becomes bytes in the platform curl's encoding (the ANSI code page with best fit on Windows, UTF-8 on Linux and macOS), encoded by the HTTP formatter with the `Encoding` `Curl.Console` puts on `HttpRequestOptions` | Accepted | 2026-09-27 |
| [0068](ADR-0068-f-form-parts-name-their-files-and-a-form-with-a-data-body-is-refused-after-the-command-line.md) | `-F` parts name their files and the transfer layer reads them; an unreadable `;headers=@file` warns `No such file or directory`; header files are UTF-8 split at LF; `-F` with a `-d` body is refused after the whole command line, as curl 8.21.0 reports it | Accepted | 2026-09-26 |
| [0069](ADR-0069-help-pages-come-from-curls-own-help-table-and-the-manual-is-embedded.md) | `-h`/`--help` pages are generated from a copy of curl 8.21.0's own help table (`CurlHelpTable`) by a port of its layout code at the terminal width, not from `CommandLineOptionTable`; the `-M` manual is the measured text, embedded; `--help <option>`, `help` in `-K` and console output are BL-374, BL-375 and BL-376 | Accepted | 2026-09-27 |
| [0070](ADR-0070-compressed-bodies-with-bytes-after-their-stream-end-exit-23-found-by-checksum-trailer.md) | With `--compressed`, bytes after the end of a gzip member, zlib stream or Brotli stream (a second gzip member included) fail with exit 23 after the stream is written, as curl 8.21.0 does; Brotli uses `BrotliDecoder`, gzip and zlib find their end from the checksum trailer (`HttpContentChecksumTrailer`); bytes after raw deflate are dropped | Accepted | 2026-09-27 |
| [0071](ADR-0071-a-failed-z-file-lookup-off-windows-stands-in-for-stat-and-prints-its-strerror-text.md) | A failed `-z` file lookup off Windows stands in for `stat` (`File.GetAttributes`) and prints curl's `strerror` text, file not found included; symbolic links and the Windows `con`/`nul` devices are follow-ups | Accepted | 2026-09-27 |
| [0072](ADR-0072-failure-messages-naming-a-host-are-cut-to-curls-255-byte-error-buffer.md) | A host `Dns` refuses as over 255 characters is not resolved (exit 6, or 5 for a proxy), and resolve failures naming a host are cut to 255 characters (`CurlErrorBuffer`), as curl 8.21.0's 256-byte error buffer cuts them; the general cut in `Curl.Console` is BL-380 | Accepted | 2026-09-27 |
| [0073](ADR-0073-a-z-date-after-the-year-9999-reads-as-the-last-second-of-9999.md) | A `-z` date after the year 9999, which curl reads in a 64-bit `time_t`, reads as 9999-12-31 23:59:59 UTC in `CurlDateParser`; `TimeCondition` stays a `DateTimeOffset`, and a date before year 1 is still refused | Accepted | 2026-09-27 |
| [0074](ADR-0074-one-curl-date-parser-lives-in-curl-protocol-abstractions.md) | One port of libcurl's `parsedate`, `CurlDateParser` in `Curl.Protocol.Abstractions`, reads `-z` dates and cookie `Expires`; `Curl.Cli` caps it into a `DateTimeOffset`, and `-z` refuses a year before 1583 | Accepted | 2026-09-27 |
| [0075](ADR-0075-the-http-handler-takes-the-transfer-timings-and-a-failed-connect-ends-them.md) | `HttpProtocolHandler` reports `TransferTimings` (start, connect, request ready and sent, first response byte, end), and a failed connect ends pretransfer, posttransfer and starttransfer as it fails, as curl 8.21.0 does; a failed connect's lookup time is a follow-up | Accepted | 2026-09-27 |
| [0076](ADR-0076-multipart-encoder-file-parts-are-encoded-as-they-are-sent.md) | Multipart `;encoder=` file parts are encoded as they are sent: `base64` and `quoted-printable` files stream through `EncodedReadStream`, a seekable `7bit` file is checked once while building and then streamed, and an unseekable `7bit` file is still read whole | Accepted; unseekable `7bit` superseded by ADR-0093 | 2026-09-27 |
| [0077](ADR-0077-proxy-headers-reach-the-connect-request-through-httpproxytunneloptions.md) | `--proxy-header` values reach the CONNECT request on `HttpProxyTunnelOptions.ProxyHeaders`, replace the curl CONNECT headers they name and follow them as measured on curl 8.21.0; `-H` values never do | Accepted | 2026-09-27 |
| [0078](ADR-0078-w-time-on-linux-and-macos-follows-glibc-strftime-in-the-c-locale.md) | `-w %time{…}` on Linux and macOS follows glibc's `strftime` in the C locale after curl's own `%f %s %z %Z %%` rewrite; `WriteOutTemplateRenderer` takes a `WriteOutTimeDialect`, which `Curl.Console` chooses by platform | Accepted | 2026-09-27 |
| [0079](ADR-0079-resolve-and-connect-to-are-kept-verbatim-by-the-parser-and-checked-at-transfer-time.md) | `--resolve` and `--connect-to` values are kept verbatim, in order, in `CommandLineOptions.ResolveEntries` / `ConnectToEntries`; the parser refuses only a missing value and the `--no-` spellings, and the syntax is checked at transfer time (exit 49), as curl 8.21.0 does | Accepted | 2026-09-27 |
| [0080](ADR-0080-retry-after-reads-the-three-rfc-9110-http-date-forms.md) | `RetryAfterHeader` reads the three RFC 9110 HTTP-date forms (RFC 1123, RFC 850, asctime) with `DateTimeOffset.TryParseExact` and any other text as delay-seconds, not libcurl's lenient `parsedate`; moving it onto `CurlDateParser` (ADR-0074) is BL-393 | Superseded by ADR-0094 | 2026-09-27 |
| [0081](ADR-0081-w-standard-output-line-feeds-follow-curls-stdout-mode-when-its-buffer-is-written.md) | `-w` standard output line feeds follow curl's stdout mode when its buffer is written: the renderer writes LF, and on Windows the runner writes standard error, and standard output while it is in text mode, through `LineFeedToCrLfStream` (numbered ADR-0040 until BL-326) | Accepted | 2026-09-26 |
| [0082](ADR-0082-the-progress-bar-replays-curls-callback-from-the-handlers-reports.md) | The `-#` bar is drawn by `ProgressBarRecorder`, which replays curl 8.21.0's `tool_progress_cb` from the handler's progress reports; a successful transfer that reported no bytes gets one last call with its byte count, as `file://` does in curl; the width is the runner's `terminalColumns` | Accepted | 2026-09-27 |
| [0083](ADR-0083-thin-socket-adapters-are-measured-by-the-integration-run.md) | `TcpDialer.DialAsync`, `UdpDatagramChannel.SendAsync` and `ReceiveAsync` are excluded from fast-run coverage and measured by the Integration run | Accepted | 2026-09-27 |
| [0084](ADR-0084-socks-handshakes-follow-the-schannel-build-of-curl-8-21-0.md) | The SOCKS handshakes follow curl 8.21.0's Schannel build: the SOCKS5 greeting offers GSSAPI without implementing it, SOCKS4 sends the first IPv4 address and SOCKS5 the first address, a literal is what `IPAddress.TryParse` accepts and prints back, text is UTF-8, and a throwing handshake disposes the proxy connection | Accepted | 2026-09-27 |
| [0085](ADR-0085-v-words-a-tls-handshake-as-the-platforms-curl-build-with-openssl-facts-on-the-event.md) | `-v` and the trace dumps word a TLS handshake as the `TlsBackend` they are given (Schannel on Windows, OpenSSL elsewhere by default); OpenSSL-only facts (group, signature type, verify code, chain) are optional properties of `TlsHandshakeEvent`, and names, dates and key sizes are ported from OpenSSL in `Curl.Output` | Accepted | 2026-09-27 |
| [0086](ADR-0086-a-repeated-transfer-started-report-starts-the-next-redirect-hops-status-line.md) | A repeated "transfer started" report starts the next redirect hop's status line; exit 47 draws the refused hop as a followed one | Accepted | 2026-09-27 |
| [0087](ADR-0087-c-dash-with-t-resumes-an-http-upload-from-an-unknown-offset.md) | `-C -` with `-T` sets `ITransferContext.ResumeUploadFromUnknownOffset`; HTTP sends the whole source with `Content-Range: bytes 0-(L-1)/L` | Accepted | 2026-09-27 |
| [0088](ADR-0088-a-socket-error-mid-handshake-is-curls-recv-failure-line.md) | A socket error during the TLS handshake is curl's `Recv failure:` line with each build's text for it (`Connection was reset` / `was aborted` in Schannel, `Connection reset by peer` in OpenSSL); others use the socket error's own message | Accepted | 2026-09-27 |
| [0089](ADR-0089-a-z-time-past-windows-gmtime-fails-the-http-transfer-with-exit-43.md) | On Windows an HTTP transfer whose `-z` time is after 3001-01-01 20:59:59 UTC (the C runtime's `gmtime` limit) fails once connected with exit 43 `Invalid TIMEVALUE` and sends nothing, as curl 8.21.0's Schannel build does; elsewhere the header is written as before | Accepted | 2026-09-27 |
| [0090](ADR-0090-the-z-stat-lookup-off-windows-follows-a-final-symbolic-link.md) | The `-z` `stat` lookup off Windows follows a final symbolic link with `File.ResolveLinkTarget`: a missing target is `No such file or directory` (or `Not a directory`), a loop `Too many levels of symbolic links`, an existing target gives its time | Accepted | 2026-09-27 |
| [0091](ADR-0091-a-failed-dial-carries-the-connectors-start-and-lookup-timings.md) | A dial that reaches no address carries `TcpConnector`'s `Started` and `NameResolved` with `ConnectTimings.Connected` (now nullable) `null`, and the HTTP handler's failed-connect report copies them, so `%{time_namelookup}` prints the lookup time and `%{time_connect}` stays `0` after a refused connect, as curl 8.21.0 does | Accepted | 2026-09-27 |
| [0092](ADR-0092-the-progress-meter-status-lines-are-drawn-from-the-handlers-byte-reports.md) | The progress meter's status lines are drawn by `TransferProgressRecorder` from the handler's byte reports and written after the transfer (written live since ADR-0099); a success that reported bytes gets three done draws, a failure one `Curl_pgrsDone` update, a handler reporting no bytes (`file://`) none; curl's `lastshow` same-second check is not modelled | Accepted | 2026-09-27 |
| [0093](ADR-0093-a-refused-multipart-byte-fails-the-send-with-exit-26.md) | A refused multipart byte fails the send with exit 26: `EncodedReadStream` throws the new `RequestBodyReadFailedException` (Abstractions), `HttpRequestBodyWriter` turns it into exit 26 `read error getting mime data` with no closing chunk, and an unseekable `7bit` file part is streamed instead of read whole; any other failed body read stays the end of the body | Accepted | 2026-09-27 |
| [0094](ADR-0094-retry-after-reads-dates-with-curldateparser.md) | `RetryAfterHeader` reads a `Retry-After` date with `CurlDateParser` (ADR-0074), so every date curl 8.21.0's `Curl_getdate_capped` reads (`27 Sep 2026 05:26:19`, `20260927 05:26:19`, a `+0100` zone) waits until it, and any other text is delay-seconds; supersedes ADR-0080 | Accepted | 2026-09-27 |
| [0095](ADR-0095-an-https-proxy-is-verified-with-its-own-tls-options.md) | An HTTPS proxy is verified with its own TLS options: `--proxy-insecure`, `--proxy-cacert` and `--proxy-capath` (else `--capath`) build a second `SslStreamTlsProvider` that `TcpConnector` runs the proxy handshake through; `-k` and `--cacert` never reach the proxy, and the one Schannel CA-path warning comes from the proxy provider | Accepted | 2026-09-27 |
| [0096](ADR-0096-tftp-through-socks-and-https-proxies-fails-as-the-reference-build-fails.md) | `tftp://` through a SOCKS proxy sends nothing and exits 97 `Send failure: Socket is not connected`; through an HTTPS proxy the MASQUE `connect-udp` request goes over TLS as through an HTTP one; for both, the proxy's reply is read: `101`/`2xx` is exit 7 `bind() failed`, another status exit 7 `CONNECT-UDP tunnel failed, response N`, a close exit 56 `Proxy CONNECT aborted` | Accepted | 2026-09-27 |
| [0097](ADR-0097-a-form-file-that-cannot-seek-declares-the-length-curls-stat-gives-it.md) | A `-F` file that cannot seek declares the length curl's `stat` gives it: on Windows a named pipe its instance count and `NUL` zero, the HTTP sender cutting the body off there; elsewhere none, so the body goes chunked. A pipe that delivers less than it declared fails with exit 26 rather than waiting | Accepted | 2026-09-27 |
| [0098](ADR-0098-an-unrewindable-form-body-on-a-redirect-fails-as-the-platforms-build-fails-it.md) | A `-F` body that cannot be rewound for a 307 or 308 hop fails as the platform's build fails it: exit 26 `read error getting mime data` on Windows, exit 65 `Cannot rewind mime/post data` elsewhere; the redirect counts and `%{redirect_url}` is empty either way | Accepted | 2026-09-27 |
| [0099](ADR-0099-the-progress-meter-is-written-live-once-the-transfer-starts.md) | The progress meter is written to standard error while the transfer runs, synchronously from the handler's reports, from the first "transfer started" report on and only when the meter is shown; the end draws, the closing newline and the meter of a transfer never reported started are written after it, so the bytes stay BL-131's | Accepted | 2026-09-27 |
| [0100](ADR-0100-the-tcp-connector-reports-its-dials-and-the-connection-it-opens.md) | `TcpConnector` reports `Trying <end point>...` before each dial, `connect to <address> port <port> from 0.0.0.0 port 0 failed: <reason>` after each failed one (curl's Winsock words on Windows, the system's message elsewhere) and the exit 7 message, and reports the connection opened, numbered from `0`, once any tunnel and TLS handshake are done; `closing connection #0` stays the handler's | Accepted | 2026-09-27 |
| [0101](ADR-0101-the-redirect-follower-sends-the-auto-referer-and-reports-the-last-one-sent.md) | Under `-e "...;auto" -L` the redirect follower sends each hop the previous URL, without user information or fragment, as its `Referer`, and `TransferReport.Referer` carries the last one sent, which `%{referer}` prints before the `-e` text (amends ADR-0015) | Accepted | 2026-09-27 |
| [0102](ADR-0102-ftp-active-mode-and-tls-need-a-listening-seam-and-four-transfer-options.md) | FTP active mode (`-P`) and TLS (`ftps://`, `--ssl`, `--ssl-reqd`) need an `IConnectionListener` seam, `IConnection.LocalEndPoint` and four transfer options, delivered by abstractions, networking, CLI and console tasks around BL-437 | Accepted | 2026-09-27 |
| [0103](ADR-0103-the-schannel-build-matches-the-common-name-of-a-cacert-certificate-without-dns-names.md) | With `--cacert` the Schannel build accepts a certificate whose subjectAltName holds no DNS name when its CN matches the host as `Curl_cert_hostcheck` matches it (one left-most `*` label, never for an IP literal); without `--cacert`, and in the OpenSSL build, .NET's name check stands | Accepted | 2026-09-27 |
| [0104](ADR-0104-a-device-under-dev-is-sent-chunked-off-windows.md) | Off Windows a seekable `-F` file under `/dev/` (not `/dev/shm/`, `/dev/fd/` or `/dev/std*`) declares no length, so the body goes chunked as libcurl sends a device; on Windows a seekable file's length stands | Accepted | 2026-09-27 |
| [0105](ADR-0105-a-failed-connect-reports-closing-connection-0.md) | The HTTP handler reports `closing connection #N` after every failed connect, with `N` from `ConnectResult.ConnectionNumber` (`0` for a failed connect), as curl 8.21.0 does after a refused dial, failed resolve or connect timeout | Accepted | 2026-09-27 |
| [0106](ADR-0106-the-runner-watches-each-attempt-for-low-speed-and-cancels-it.md) | `Curl.Core`'s `LowSpeedWatchdog` checks each attempt's speed once a second as `Curl_speedcheck` does and cancels it through `ITransferContext.CancellationToken` after `-y` seconds below `-Y` (30 seconds, or 1 byte per second, when only one is given); the runner turns that into exit 28 and curl's message | Accepted | 2026-09-27 |
| [0107](ADR-0107-ftp-active-mode-retries-a-non-local-port-address-in-the-handler.md) | FTP active mode retries a `-P` address that is not local once on the control connection's address in the handler, as curl 8.21.0's `ftp_port_bind_socket` does; `TcpConnectionListener` words `EADDRNOTAVAIL` as curl's `-v` line so the handler can tell it apart, and `EPRT`/`PORT` still announce the `-P` address | Accepted | 2026-09-27 |
| [0108](ADR-0108-ftp-active-mode-resolves-a-port-name-through-the-injected-dns-resolver.md) | FTP active mode resolves a `-P` name through an injected `IDnsResolver` and listens on its first address, IPv4-mapped announced as IPv4; a handler built without a resolver ends every name with exit 6 | Accepted | 2026-09-27 |
| [0109](ADR-0109-a-failed-connect-takes-the-next-connection-number.md) | A failed connect takes the next connection number: `ConnectResult.Failed` and `Refused` carry `ConnectionNumber`, `TcpConnector` numbers every connect that gets past its options and `PoolingConnector` numbers the inner connector's failures, so `closing connection #N` counts as curl 8.21.0 does | Accepted | 2026-09-27 |
| [0110](ADR-0110-ftp-active-mode-looks-up-a-port-name-as-a-network-interface-off-windows.md) | FTP active mode looks up a `-P` name as a network interface off Windows through the new `INetworkInterfaceLookup` seam (`SystemNetworkInterfaceLookup` over `NetworkInterface`, finding nothing on Windows) before resolving it as a host | Accepted | 2026-09-27 |
| [0111](ADR-0111-the-progress-sink-hears-when-the-transfer-is-done-before-the-connection-end-line.md) | `ITransferProgress.ReportTransferDone()` (default body empty) tells the progress sink the data is complete; the HTTP handler calls it once, for the final exchange, before the connection-end `-v` line, as curl 8.21.0 calls `Curl_pgrsDone` before `multi_done` writes it. Amends ADR-0045 | Accepted | 2026-09-27 |
| [0112](ADR-0112-the-pool-reports-a-connection-whose-close-was-read-as-dead.md) | `PooledConnection` notes a read that found the server's close; `PoolingConnector` reports such an idle connection with `Connection N seems to be dead` and `shutting down connection #N` and closes it instead of reusing it, and the HTTP handler marks every connection it reports `left intact` reusable, as curl 8.21.0 does. No socket peek: curl reuses an idle-closed connection it read no close on. Amends ADR-0109 | Accepted | 2026-09-27 |
| [0113](ADR-0113-the-tcp-connector-keeps-curl-s-dns-cache-for-the-command-line.md) | `TcpConnector` keeps curl 8.21.0's DNS cache for the command line, keyed on the host and port resolved (a `--connect-to` destination, the proxy, a SOCKS target): a `--resolve` entry or a key resolved before answers without the resolver and reports `Hostname H was found in DNS cache` before `Trying`; a failed dial stays cached, a failed lookup does not | Accepted | 2026-09-27 |
| [0114](ADR-0114-the-tcp-connector-loads-resolve-entries-per-transfer-and-reports-what-it-resolved.md) | `TcpConnector.LoadResolveEntries`, for the console at each transfer's start, and otherwise the first connect, loads the `--resolve` entries (`ResolveOverrides.Entries`) into its DNS cache, reporting `Added H:P:A to DNS cache` and `RESOLVE H:P - old addresses discarded`, and follows every answer with curl's `Host H:P was resolved.`, `IPv6:` and `IPv4:` lines, naming the cached name; none for an IP address. Amends ADR-0113 | Accepted | 2026-09-27 |
| [0115](ADR-0115-the-parser-applies-the-speed-defaults-in-command-line-order.md) | The `-Y` and `-y` appliers apply curl 8.21.0's speed defaults as they parse: `-Y` makes a zero speed time 30 and `-y` makes a zero speed limit 1, so `-Y 0 -y 2` watches for 1 byte per second while `-y 2 -Y 0` and `-Y 100 -y 0` watch nothing. Amends ADR-0106 | Accepted | 2026-09-27 |
| [0116](ADR-0116-the-console-holds-the-v-lines-from-transfer-done-until-the-meter-ends.md) | Under a live progress meter the runner holds the `-v` and trace output from the handler's `ReportTransferDone` until it has written the meter's end for the transfer's result, then releases it, so the connection-end line follows the meter as in curl 8.21.0, after a success or a failure. Builds on ADR-0099 and ADR-0111 | Accepted | 2026-09-27 |
| [0117](ADR-0117-the-connector-owns-the-connect-timeout-and-the-runner-owns-max-time.md) | Every scheme times out with exit 28: `TcpConnector` enforces `--connect-timeout` (300 seconds by default) over resolve, dial, tunnel and handshake, and a per-attempt `MaxTimeWatchdog` in the runner enforces `-m`, taking the byte counts for curl's message from `ITransferProgress`. HTTP and TFTP keep their own enforcement; a new handler only honours the token and reports progress. Builds on ADR-0008, ADR-0040 and ADR-0106 | Accepted | 2026-09-28 |
| [0118](ADR-0118-curl-cryptography-hand-builds-every-primitive-the-bcl-lacks-on-a-ci-platform.md) | A primitive comes from `System.Security.Cryptography` only when the BCL has it on Windows, Linux and macOS; otherwise `Curl.Cryptography.UnitLibrary` hand-builds it once for every platform (X25519, Ed25519, ChaCha20-Poly1305, Blowfish, bcrypt-pbkdf, MD4, RIPEMD-160, RC4, CAST-128, HPKE, AES-CTR and CTS, AES-CCM, finite-field DH, X448, Ed448, brainpool, SHA-3, ML-KEM, ML-DSA, DSA; sntrup761 added by ADR-0122), span-based, one public type per primitive in `Curl.Cryptography`, constant-time and zeroing its secrets, pinned by published vectors | Accepted | 2026-09-28 |
| [0119](ADR-0119-the-composition-records-each-transfers-first-connection-for-the-endpoint-variables.md) | `%{local_ip}`, `%{local_port}`, `%{remote_ip}` and `%{remote_port}` come from the first connection each transfer opens, recorded by connector decorators and put on the report by a handler decorator in `CurlComposition` when the handler reported neither; HTTP keeps its own. `%{local_port}` is `0` for a connection with no local end (TFTP) and `-1` for none | Accepted | 2026-09-28 |
| [0120](ADR-0120-protocol-libraries-may-reference-the-hand-built-libraries-and-nothing-else-horizontal.md) | A `Curl.Protocol.*.UnitLibrary` may reference Abstractions and the hand-built libraries (`Curl.Cryptography`, `Curl.Ntlm`, `Curl.Kerberos`, `Curl.Tls`, `Curl.Http2`, `Curl.Quic`, `Curl.Http3`, and `Curl.Zstandard` by ADR-0185), each of which references only the BCL and the lower layers its row names, never a protocol, `Curl.Networking`, `Curl.Core` or `Curl.Console`, and never a socket; enforced by `ProtocolIsolationTests` (BL-668); a new hand-built library joins by amending this ADR | Accepted | 2026-09-28 |
| [0121](ADR-0121-the-mail-handlers-share-an-injected-sasl-authenticator-and-one-mail-options-record.md) | SMTP, POP3 and IMAP share an `ISaslAuthenticator` declared in Abstractions and implemented in `Curl.Authentication` (ranking measured: EXTERNAL on request, GSSAPI with a domain user, DIGEST-MD5, CRAM-MD5, NTLM, OAUTHBEARER, XOAUTH2, LOGIN, PLAIN), take their options from one `ITransferContext.Mail` record, upgrade with STARTTLS/STLS through `ITlsProvider` by `SslLevel` (exit 64 when required and not offered), read their own URLs and each keep their own line reader | Accepted | 2026-09-28 |
| [0122](ADR-0122-scp-and-sftp-offer-each-platform-curls-libssh2-algorithms-and-build-every-libssh2-and-libssh-algorithm.md) | `scp` and `sftp` send `SSH-2.0-libssh2_1.11.1` and a `KEXINIT` identical to the platform curl's (measured: the WinCNG list on Windows, the OpenSSL list on Linux and macOS, narrowed by a known-hosts entry as curl narrows it, exit 79 for a type the preset lacks), while every algorithm libssh2 1.11.1 and libssh 0.12.2 offer is built, from the BCL or `Curl.Cryptography` (sntrup761 added to ADR-0118), in a `Full` preset; `--key` reads PEM, PKCS #8 and `openssh-key-v1`, encrypted or not, for RSA, DSA, ECDSA and Ed25519; options on `ITransferContext.Ssh`; tested against an in-memory SSH peer | Accepted | 2026-09-28 |
| [0123](ADR-0123-sasl-exchanges-carry-every-initial-response-and-plain-outranks-login.md) | Measured: PLAIN outranks LOGIN (correcting ADR-0121 §2), an authorization identity does not skip LOGIN, a bearer token excludes PLAIN and LOGIN; every built mechanism has an initial response (LOGIN's the user name), sent inline under `--sasl-ir` or in answer to the first challenge; OAUTHBEARER's `port=` waits for `SaslRequest` to carry the port | Accepted | 2026-09-28 |
| [0124](ADR-0124-the-connector-honours-no-alpn-no-keepalive-no-tcp-nodelay-and-ssl-revoke-best-effort.md) | Measured: an `https://` origin handshake offers `http/1.1` through ALPN on every platform (the OpenSSL build's `h2` waits for BL-659) and `--no-alpn` offers none; `TcpDialer` sets `TCP_NODELAY` and `SO_KEEPALIVE` (60 s) unless `--no-tcp-nodelay` or `--no-keepalive`; the Schannel build's `--ssl-revoke-best-effort` accepts a `--cacert` chain whose only faults are an unknown or offline revocation status; `--ca-native` changes nothing on either build | Accepted | 2026-09-28 |
| [0125](ADR-0125-next-option-groups-share-one-global-state-and-refuse-later-groups-after-earlier-ones-run.md) | Every `--next` group is a `CommandLineOptions` sharing one `CommandLineGlobalState` for the global options (`CommandLineOptionTable.GlobalOptionLongNames`, checked by a test); `CommandLineParseResult.Groups` lists the groups to run, and a later group's setup refusal is `RefusalAfterGroups`, printed after the earlier groups run; a missing password prompt ends ` on URL #<n>:` with more than one group | Accepted | 2026-09-28 |
| [0126](ADR-0126-next-option-groups-run-in-order-with-a-dispatch-each-sharing-cookies-and-numbering.md) | Measured: `CurlCommandRunner` runs the `--next` groups in order, each with its own `TransferDispatch` built from its own options; `%{urlnum}`, `%{xfer_id}` and `%{conn_id}` count on across groups; one `CookieStore` per run with each group's `CookieEngine` keeping its own `-b`, `-c` and `-j`; the exit code is the last transfer's | Accepted | 2026-09-28 |
| [0127](ADR-0127-parallel-transfers-write-in-completion-order-and-exit-with-the-first-failure.md) | Measured: under `-Z` up to `--parallel-max` transfers run as tasks from one command-line-ordered queue across all `--next` groups; bodies, error lines and `-w` text reach the shared streams in completion order under one write gate; the run exits with the first failure's code in completion order (serial keeps the last); `--fail-early` aborts running transfers with 42 and ends queued ones with the first failure's code; `CookieStore` (BL-755) and the runner's per-run fields are made safe for concurrent use | Accepted | 2026-09-28 |
| [0128](ADR-0128-the-websocket-library-writes-its-own-upgrade-request-and-reads-its-own-101-head.md) | Measured: `Curl.Protocol.Ws.UnitLibrary` writes curl's upgrade request and reads the `101` head itself (no Abstractions change); `-H`, `-A`, `-e`, `-X`, pre-emptive `-u`, proxies (always tunnelled), `-D`, `-T` (one binary frame) apply, `-b`, `--compressed`, `-d` and `-i` do not; data and close payloads are written, pings answered with a pong, the transfer ends when the server closes; any status but `101` exits 22; `Sec-WebSocket-Accept` is not checked; key and masks come from an injected `IWebSocketRandomSource` | Accepted | 2026-09-28 |
| [0129](ADR-0129-the-live-task-board-page-reads-the-task-tree-and-a-board-branch-status-json.md) | The live task board page (`.github/board/site/index.html`, served at `/Curl/board/`) draws the kanban from one recursive tree call on `work/dark-factory`, titles from file names with no index, and lane cards from `status.json` (schema 1) on a one-commit `board` branch that only the coordinator force-pushes from the lanes' local heartbeat files; refresh is 60 s per API call | Accepted | 2026-09-28 |
| [0130](ADR-0130-lanes-auto-paces-dark-factory-lanes-to-the-usage-windows-the-board-and-the-machine.md) | `RunDarkFactory.ps1 -Lanes Auto` (or 1 to 16 fixed) meters each lane's burn of the five-hour and weekly windows every 15 minutes, paces to `min(fiveHourTarget, weeklyTarget)` under the ceiling `min(board capacity, probed machine cap, -MaxLanes)`, adds one lane per step and retires straight down to the pace with hysteresis, retires lanes only between tasks, starts at its ceiling (`auto-lanes.json` raised to `-MinStartLanes`, default 16), probes into `machine-lanes.json`, traces `lanes <old> -> <new> (<limit>)` and publishes `autoLanes` in `status.json` | Accepted | 2026-09-28 |
| [0131](ADR-0131-the-websocket-frame-reader-streams-payloads-answers-the-last-ping-per-read-and-fails-violations-with-56.md) | Measured: the frame reader keeps its place across reads and writes text, binary, continuation, close and pong payloads as they arrive (a cut-short frame's bytes too); each ping gets a masked pong, only the last one per read; a close is neither answered nor the end; reserved bits, unknown opcodes, fragment-order errors, masked, fragmented or oversized control frames and 64-bit lengths with the top bit fail with 56 and curl's `[WS]` message | Accepted | 2026-09-28 |
| [0132](ADR-0132-imap-fetch-follows-curl-and-drops-the-connection-a-transfer-failed-on.md) | Measured: an IMAP URL naming a mailbox and a `UID` or `MAILINDEX` sends `SELECT` (the name quoted as curl's `imap_atom` quotes it) then `UID FETCH`/`FETCH ... BODY[section]<partial>` and streams the literal to the output; command-phase failures (exit 3, 67, 78, 8) send `LOGOUT` first, a failure inside the literal (exit 18, 23) does not; a read failure mid-literal counts as the server closing | Accepted | 2026-09-28 |
| [0133](ADR-0133-smtp-auth-follows-curls-framing-and-fails-every-refusal-with-67.md) | Measured: SMTP offers the words after `AUTH ` on every `EHLO` line; authenticates only with `-u`, a bearer token or `AUTH=EXTERNAL`; puts the initial response on the `AUTH` line under `--sasl-ir` within 504 characters; answers each `334` and fails any other reply, an early `235` or an unanswerable challenge with 67 `Login denied` and nothing more sent; ADR-0123's last-mechanism quirk does not reproduce | Accepted | 2026-09-28 |
| [0134](ADR-0134-pop3-logs-in-with-sasl-then-apop-then-user-and-pass-as-measured.md) | Measured: POP3 logs in with SASL `AUTH` when CAPA offers a chosen mechanism, else `APOP` when the greeting has a timestamp, else `USER`/`PASS` when CAPA lists `USER` or is refused, else 67 `Login denied`; `AUTH=+APOP` and `AUTH=<mech>` restrict the choice, unknown options are exit 3; a non-base64 challenge is empty, `+OK` succeeds once the initial response is sent | Accepted | 2026-09-28 |
| [0135](ADR-0135-smtp-without-a-message-sends-vrfy-expn-help-or-the-x-command-and-writes-each-reply-line.md) | Measured: SMTP without an upload or a recipient sends `VRFY` per recipient (bare address, IDNA host, ` SMTPUTF8` when needed), the `-X` command per recipient or alone (` SMTPUTF8` only on `EXPN`), or `HELP`; writes each reply line as it came, the final one only when 2xx (or 553 for a recipient); a refusal is 8 `Command failed: <code>` and stops the loop | Accepted | 2026-09-28 |
| [0136](ADR-0136-smtp-mail-from-adds-auth-size-and-smtputf8-and-allowfails-carries-on-past-refused-recipients.md) | Measured: SMTP `MAIL FROM` adds ` AUTH=<addr>` once `AUTH` succeeded, ` SIZE=<n>` when advertised and the upload has a known, non-zero length, and ` SMTPUTF8` when advertised and any address is non-ASCII; hosts become A-labels; `SIZE`/`SMTPUTF8` match in any case; `--mail-rcpt-allowfails` passes over refused recipients and fails only when all are, 55 `RCPT failed: <code> (last error)` | Accepted | 2026-09-28 |
| [0137](ADR-0137-a-real-curl-option-not-yet-implemented-is-refused-as-the-installed-libcurl-does-not-support-it.md) | Four option classes told apart by `CommandLineOptionTable` and `CurlOptionAliasTable`: parsed; deprecated with no function (warning, transfer runs); real but not yet implemented (`the installed libcurl version does not support this`, exit 2, `-K` lines alike); not a curl option (`is unknown`, exit 2); the unimplemented list is derived, never hand-written | Accepted | 2026-09-28 |
| [0138](ADR-0138-tls-version-range-min-to-tls-max-refused-at-parse-when-empty-and-kept-off-the-proxy.md) | Measured: the handshake offers every TLS version from the minimum (`-1`, `--tlsv1.x`) to `--tls-max` (`TlsVersion`, `TlsVersionRange`); an empty range is refused while parsing with curl's two lines, exit 2; a ceiling of TLS 1.0/1.1 fails as `schannel: failed to receive handshake` or OpenSSL's `no protocols available`, exit 35; an HTTPS proxy gets `--proxy-tlsv1` only | Accepted | 2026-09-28 |
| [0139](ADR-0139-sasl-cram-md5-and-digest-md5-answer-as-each-platforms-curl-with-sspi-on-windows.md) | Measured: SASL ranks DIGEST-MD5, then CRAM-MD5, above PLAIN, only with a user and no token; CRAM-MD5 is `user <hex HMAC-MD5>`; DIGEST-MD5 answers as the platform's curl - SSPI's message on Windows (domain as realm, bare `nc`, `charset=utf-8` echoed), curl's own elsewhere - then an empty line to `rspauth`; SSPI rejecting a challenge fails with exit 94 through `SaslAuthenticationFailedException` (BL-781 amendment) | Accepted | 2026-09-28 |
| [0140](ADR-0140-the-hand-built-tls-client-runs-quic-always-and-tcp-only-where-sslstream-cannot.md) | Measured ClientHellos: `Curl.Tls.UnitLibrary`'s hand-built TLS 1.0 to 1.3 client runs every QUIC handshake and a TCP one only when an option `SslStream` cannot honour is in force (one routing function, a row per option); it sends the platform curl's measured ClientHello (Schannel, OpenSSL 3.5.5, LibreSSL for QUIC on Windows), supports every suite, group and extension an official build offers, and hands the chain to the one existing verifier | Accepted | 2026-09-28 |
| [0141](ADR-0141-http-2-is-hand-built-and-accepted-everywhere-with-h2-offered-by-default-off-windows.md) | Measured: HTTP/2 is hand-built in `Curl.Http2.UnitLibrary`; `--http2`, `--http2-prior-knowledge`, `--http1.1` and `--http1.0` are accepted on every platform with nghttp2 curl's ALPN, `h2c` upgrade and `-v` lines; a plain `https://` offers `h2,http/1.1` on Linux and macOS and `http/1.1` alone on Windows, as the Schannel reference does; `-V` lists `HTTP2` everywhere; supersedes ADR-0017 for HTTP/2 | Accepted | 2026-09-28 |
| [0142](ADR-0142-ntlm-negotiate-and-kerberos-answer-through-sspi-on-windows-and-curls-own-code-or-the-system-gss-api-elsewhere.md) | Measured: Windows answers NTLM, Negotiate and Kerberos through `NegotiateAuthentication` over SSPI (byte for byte curl's SSPI Type 1); Linux and macOS answer NTLM with hand-built `Curl.Ntlm` (curl's own Type 1) and Negotiate/Kerberos through the system GSS-API, falling back to hand-built SPNEGO and `Curl.Kerberos` only when it answers `Unsupported`; SMB uses hand-built NTLM everywhere; one `ISecurityContextFactory` seam in Abstractions; `--krb` has no function, as in both platform curls | Accepted | 2026-09-28 |
| [0143](ADR-0143-4-and-6-filter-resolved-names-and-leave-address-literals-alone.md) | Measured: under `-4` or `-6` the TCP and UDP connectors dial a host or proxy name at that family's addresses only, and fail one with none as not resolved (exit 6, exit 5 for a proxy); an IP address literal is dialled as written; a looked-up answer is reported with the one family, `localhost` and `--resolve` entries whole, and an entry left empty as `Negative DNS entry` | Accepted | 2026-09-28 |
| [0144](ADR-0144-http-3-is-hand-built-over-a-hand-built-quic-and-http3-races-tcp-as-curls-ngtcp2-build-does.md) | Measured: HTTP/3 is hand-built in `Curl.Http3` over a hand-built QUIC v1 in `Curl.Quic` on every platform, with curl.se's ngtcp2 build's transport parameters, ALPN `h3,h3-29` and CUBIC; `--http3` races QUIC against TCP (`h2,http/1.1`) and falls back at `--happy-eyeballs-timeout-ms` or on failure, `--http3-only` never falls back; new `IMultiplexedConnection` contracts over `IDatagramChannel`; exits 3, 7, 8, 28, 55, 56, 60, 95 and 96 mapped; supersedes ADR-0017 for HTTP/3 | Accepted | 2026-09-28 |
| [0145](ADR-0145-camellia-indexes-its-fixed-s-boxes-and-is-not-constant-time.md) | `Camellia` in `Curl.Cryptography` indexes RFC 3713's fixed S-boxes directly, as OpenSSL's and LibreSSL's Camellia do, and says in its XML docs that it is not constant-time; amends ADR-0118's exception list to Blowfish, CAST-128, RC4 and Camellia | Accepted | 2026-09-28 |
| [0146](ADR-0146-the-tls-1-3-client-handshake-takes-its-key-shares-and-extension-order-from-its-caller.md) | `Tls13ClientHandshake` takes its ephemeral key shares from `ITlsRandomSource.CreateKeyShare` and its ClientHello extension order from `Tls13ClientSettings` (built extensions in place, the rest verbatim, NSS/BoringSSL padding to 512); NIST points are checked on the curve by hand; failures are returned as `TlsHandshakeFailure` with RFC 8446's alerts; after completion only NewSessionTicket is taken | Accepted | 2026-09-28 |
| [0147](ADR-0147-aria-indexes-its-fixed-s-boxes-and-is-not-constant-time-while-its-ghash-is.md) | `Aria` in `Curl.Cryptography` indexes RFC 5794's fixed S-boxes directly, as OpenSSL's ARIA does, and is not constant-time; GCM is built once in the internal `GaloisCounterMode` with a constant-time bitwise GHASH, a 12-byte nonce and a 16-byte tag, runs under `AeadAriaGcm`, and is checked against the BCL's `AesGcm` over AES; amends ADR-0118's exception list to Blowfish, CAST-128, RC4, Camellia and ARIA | Accepted | 2026-09-28 |
| [0148](ADR-0148-the-hpack-encoder-chooses-representations-as-nghttp2s-deflater-does.md) | `HpackEncoder` chooses index, literal kind and Huffman as nghttp2's deflater does (never-indexed credentials and short cookies, unindexed `:path` and friends, Huffman only when shorter, a 4096-byte ceiling), pinned to the header block curl 8.18.0 sent; `HpackDecoder` reports each broken RFC 7541 rule as its own `HpackDecodingError` | Accepted | 2026-09-28 |
| [0149](ADR-0149-unix-socket-connects-replace-the-host-and-proxy-and-follow-curl-8-21-0s-windows-wording-everywhere.md) | Measured: under `--unix-socket` or `--abstract-unix-socket` `TcpConnector` dials that socket in place of the host, port and proxy (the proxy dropped unread), with curl 8.21.0's Windows `-v` lines and exit 7 message on every platform, the path cut to 45 characters as the address, exit 6 for a path too long for `sun_path`; pools stay per option group | Accepted | 2026-09-28 |
| [0150](ADR-0150-tls-1-0-cbc-inserts-openssls-empty-fragment-and-cbc-records-fail-bad-padding-and-bad-macs-alike.md) | `Tls12RecordWriteState` answers BEAST as OpenSSL and LibreSSL do, with an empty application data record before each TLS 1.0 CBC write (off for `--ssl-allow-beast`), not Schannel's 1/n-1 split; CBC padding is checked with masks and bad padding, a bad MAC and a bad length are all `bad_record_mac` by one branch, and since BL-795 the MAC is a fixed-block HMAC, so the Lucky Thirteen hash-time residual is gone; GCM's explicit nonce is the sequence number | Accepted | 2026-09-28 |
| [0151](ADR-0151-the-ten-tls-options-are-accepted-everywhere-and-run-on-the-hand-built-client-with-the-honouring-builds-text.md) | `--curves`, `--sigalgs`, `--tls-earlydata`, `--ech`, `--ssl-sessions`, TLS-SRP, `--no-sessionid`, `--ssl-allow-beast` and TLS 1.0/1.1 are accepted on every platform and run on the hand-built TLS client with the text of the build that honours them; `--engine list` prints `<none>`, `--engine <name>` is ignored on Windows and fails 53 or 66 elsewhere, and `--dump-ca-embed` prints nothing because Curl embeds no CA bundle; measured on the Schannel, curl.se and OpenSSL builds | Accepted | 2026-09-28 |
| [0152](ADR-0152-dns-over-https-posts-a-and-aaaa-in-parallel-over-a-minimal-http-1-1-exchange-inside-networking.md) | `DohDnsResolver` lives in `Curl.Networking.UnitLibrary` with its own minimal HTTP/1.1 POST exchange (not the HTTP protocol library, not `HttpClient`); it POSTs the A and AAAA queries in parallel on two connections with curl's measured five header lines and no `User-Agent`; only `--doh-insecure` and `--doh-cert-status` reach the DoH TLS, never `-k`; every DoH failure leaves the name unresolved, exit 6; measured on the Schannel build | Accepted | 2026-09-28 |
| [0153](ADR-0153-parallel-transfers-to-one-http-host-wait-for-the-first-to-end-unless-parallel-immediate.md) | Under `-Z`, `ParallelHostQueue` holds a transfer back inside its `--parallel-max` slot while `--parallel-max-host` transfers run to its `host:port`, and, without `--parallel-immediate`, an `http://` transfer waits until the first to its host has ended, as curl 8.21.0's pipe-wait does over HTTP/1.1; measured on the Schannel build | Accepted | 2026-09-28 |
| [0154](ADR-0154-the-tls-1-2-client-handshake-refuses-legacy-renegotiation-ignores-hello-request-and-matches-openssls-alerts.md) | `Tls12ClientHandshake` is I/O-free, with the ChangeCipherSpec sent and taken as its own message and the key block handed to the caller; it refuses a server without secure renegotiation with `handshake_failure` as OpenSSL does, ignores HelloRequest, refuses DHE under 1024 bits, offers a ticket with a random session ID, checks TLS 1.0/1.1 RSA signatures with the public operation and sends an empty Certificate for an RSA client key there until the private operation is built, and takes each alert from OpenSSL's client | Accepted | 2026-09-28 |
| [0155](ADR-0155-a-parallel-run-draws-curls-combined-progress-meter-on-its-loops-events.md) | Under `-Z`, `ParallelProgressMeter` draws curl 8.21.0's combined meter (`ParallelProgressMeterText`) in place of each transfer's own, on a byte report, a transfer ending, the runner having started what it can, or a second idle, at most every 500 ms, then a final line; `-#` is ignored and `-s` and `--no-progress-meter` hide it; measured on the Schannel build | Accepted | 2026-09-28 |
| [0156](ADR-0156-ntlm-hand-builds-des-and-answers-as-curl-8-21-0-does.md) | `Curl.Cryptography.Des` is hand-built because the BCL's `DES` refuses the weak key an empty password's LM hash needs; `Curl.Ntlm` hashes strings as curl 8.21.0 does (UTF-8 bytes widened, ASCII-only uppercasing), computes NTLMv1, NTLMv1 with extended session security and NTLMv2 with their session keys, and `NtlmChallengeAnswerer` sends NTLMv2 only when the challenge sets extended session security, NTLMv1 and LM otherwise; amends ADR-0118's not-constant-time list with DES | Accepted | 2026-09-28 |
| [0157](ADR-0157-the-tls-1-3-client-stream-ends-quietly-without-close-notify-and-its-caller-answers-as-curl.md) | `Tls13ClientStream` returns 0 at `close_notify` or at a transport that ends without one, as `SslStream` does, and `CloseNotifyReceived` lets its caller fail an unfinished transfer with curl's exit 56; records are never padded, the first ClientHello record carries 0x0301, compatibility mode sends one `change_cipher_spec`, a requested KeyUpdate is answered before the write keys move on; the CCM suites wait for BL-811 | Accepted | 2026-09-28 |
| [0158](ADR-0158-the-hand-built-kerberos-reads-version-4-credential-caches-and-version-2-keytabs.md) | The hand-built Kerberos reads version 4 credential caches and version 2 keytabs through `IKerberosFileReader`, finds the default cache as MIT does (`KRB5CCNAME`, then `FILE:/tmp/krb5cc_<uid>`), reads only `FILE:` (and `WRFILE:` keytabs), and fails with a typed `KerberosFileError` | Accepted | 2026-09-28 |
| [0159](ADR-0159-an-http-2-stream-is-read-and-written-as-the-http-1-exchange-curls-own-h2-layer-presents.md) | Measured: the HTTP handler speaks HTTP/2 (with `Http2PriorKnowledge`, or when the connect reports ALPN `h2`) through `Http2StreamConnection`, one stream presented as the HTTP/1 exchange curl's own h2 layer presents - HEADERS from the HTTP/1 head (pseudo-headers, lower-cased names, connection-specific ones dropped), `HTTP/2 200 \r\n` heads, trailers with no empty line - so every HTTP/1.1 path is shared; RST_STREAM is exit 92, protocol errors 16, GOAWAY 56, a close mid-body 18; HTTP/2 connections are not pooled yet | Accepted | 2026-09-28 |
| [0160](ADR-0160-the-hand-built-kerberos-reads-krb5-conf-as-mits-profile-library-and-locates-kdcs-from-the-file-then-srv.md) | The hand-built Kerberos reads `krb5.conf` as MIT's profile library does (`KRB5_CONFIG`, then `/etc/krb5.conf`; `include`/`includedir`; a malformed line fails the file with a typed `KerberosConfigurationError`), maps a host to its realm through `[domain_realm]` then `default_realm`, and locates KDCs from `kdc` entries, then `_kerberos._udp`/`_tcp` SRV records ordered by priority and weight | Accepted | 2026-09-28 |
| [0161](ADR-0161-the-hand-built-kerberos-encrypts-with-aes-sha1-aes-sha2-and-rc4-hmac-from-the-initial-cipher-state.md) | The hand-built Kerberos encrypts, decrypts, checksums and makes keys with `aes128`/`aes256-cts-hmac-sha1-96`, `aes128-cts-hmac-sha256-128`, `aes256-cts-hmac-sha384-192` and `rc4-hmac` through one `KerberosEncryption` per type, from the initial cipher state only, with typed `KerberosCryptographyError` failures and MIT's 2^24 iteration limit | Accepted | 2026-09-28 |
| [0162](ADR-0162-the-hand-built-tls-client-is-offered-through-itlsprovider-with-the-sslstream-providers-verifier-and-text.md) | `TlsClientRouting.Choose` routes a `--tls-max` of TLS 1.0 or 1.1 (ADR-0140's only row whose option exists yet) to `HandBuiltTlsProvider` and everything else to `SslStreamTlsProvider`; both share `ServerCertificateVerification`, so exit 60 and 77 and their text are the same; a range reaching TLS 1.3 runs the TLS 1.3 client, a lower ceiling the TLS 1.2/1.1/1.0 one; other handshake failures are exit 35 with Schannel's measured lines or OpenSSL's alert error strings; exit 56 without `close_notify` is follow-up work for both paths | Accepted | 2026-09-28 |
| [0163](ADR-0163-the-hand-built-kerberos-encodes-messages-in-der-with-system-formats-asn1-and-reads-ber-with-typed-failures.md) | The hand-built Kerberos encodes AS-REQ, TGS-REQ, AP-REQ and the replies and KRB-ERROR in DER with `System.Formats.Asn1` and reads BER, with typed `KerberosMessageError` failures, UTF-8 GeneralStrings, 32-bit flags, signed `UInt32`s accepted, and `EncKDCRepPart`'s tag kept as read | Accepted | 2026-09-28 |
| [0164](ADR-0164-the-qpack-encoder-inserts-only-while-a-stream-may-block-and-the-decoder-coalesces-its-acknowledgements.md) | `QpackEncoder` prefers a static exact match, then a dynamic exact match, then a new insert, then a static, dynamic or literal name; it references or inserts unacknowledged entries only while the stream may block, never evicts an entry the decoder may still need, uses Base 0 for a section with no dynamic reference and Huffman-codes when shorter; `QpackDecoder` acknowledges each section with dynamic references, coalesces Insert Count Increments until its bytes are taken, and fails a Required Insert Count its references do not need | Accepted | 2026-09-28 |
| [0165](ADR-0165-the-quic-client-handshake-is-an-io-free-state-machine-that-maps-each-failure-to-one-exit.md) | The QUIC client handshake is an I/O-free `QuicClientHandshake` driven over `IDatagramChannel` by `QuicClientConnector`; TLS reports the server's `quic_transport_parameters`; curl's measured ClientHello less the `00ff` value; Version Negotiation and server closes are exit 7 (`CONNECTION_REFUSED` 8), transport-parameter errors 7, a rejected certificate 60, any other TLS failure 35, the 10-second handshake timeout 55 and `--connect-timeout` 28 | Accepted | 2026-09-28 |
| [0166](ADR-0166-ldap-is-hand-built-on-iconnection-and-answers-as-each-platforms-winldap-or-openldap-curl-build.md) | LDAP is hand-built on `IConnection`: replies read with `System.Formats.Asn1`, requests written by a hand-built BER writer, and an `LdapDialect` (`WinLdap` on Windows, `OpenLdap` elsewhere) choosing the measured length form, default filter spelling, LDAPv2 bind retry, error texts, exits (38, 39, 67) and output lines; RFC 4516 URLs, RFC 4515 filters | Accepted | 2026-09-28 |
| [0167](ADR-0167-the-hand-built-spnego-offers-kerberos-v5-alone-as-mit-does-and-reads-negtokenresp-with-typed-failures.md) | The hand-built SPNEGO wraps a NegTokenInit of `mechTypes` and `mechToken` only in RFC 2743's framing and offers Kerberos V5 alone, as curl with MIT krb5 1.22.1 was measured to; `SpnegoNegotiationResponse` reads the bare `[1]` NegTokenResp in BER with every field optional and typed `Malformed` or `UnexpectedToken` failures | Accepted | 2026-09-28 |
| [0168](ADR-0168-the-hand-built-kerberos-gets-tickets-from-the-cache-then-tgs-or-from-a-password-by-as-with-encrypted-timestamp.md) | `KerberosKdcClient` gets a service ticket from the credential cache or by TGS with its live TGT, or from a password by AS (`PA-ENC-TIMESTAMP` after `KDC_ERR_PREAUTH_REQUIRED`, salted by `PA-ETYPE-INFO2`) then TGS; offers aes256/aes128-sha1, aes256-sha384, aes128-sha256 and rc4; UDP within `udp_preference_limit`, TCP otherwise or after `KRB_ERR_RESPONSE_TOO_BIG`; every KRB-ERROR and wrong reply a typed `KerberosKdcException`; referrals and MS-KKDCP left to their own tasks | Accepted | 2026-09-28 |
| [0169](ADR-0169-the-rtsp-library-writes-its-own-options-request-and-reads-its-own-reply-with-cseq-checked-per-transfer.md) | Measured: `Curl.Protocol.Rtsp.UnitLibrary` writes its own request and reads its own reply (no Abstractions change); the tool sends one `OPTIONS * RTSP/1.0` per transfer whatever `-X` says, headers `CSeq`, `Referer`, `User-Agent`, `Authorization`, `-H`; the reply body is never written, the head only with `-i`/`-I`/`-D`; a wrong or missing `CSeq` exits 85, `-f` fails 400+ with 22, a non-RTSP reply 52; `CSeq` and `Session` are per transfer (a reused connection's transfer sends `CSeq: 0`), and exit 86 is pinned at the handler level because the tool cannot reach it | Accepted | 2026-09-28 |
| [0170](ADR-0170-dns-servers-resolve-through-a-hand-built-c-ares-style-client-inside-networking.md) | `--dns-servers`, `--dns-interface`, `--dns-ipv4-addr` and `--dns-ipv6-addr` switch the run to `DnsServerResolver`, a hand-built client that reuses the DoH codec (SRV added), sends AAAA and A at once over UDP with c-ares 1.34.8's EDNS cookie query, retries over TCP on truncation, tries the servers in order for three rounds of 2, 4 and 8 s without jitter, binds each socket by the options, and reports c-ares' reason after `Could not resolve host:` or exit 43 for a list or address that does not parse | Accepted | 2026-09-28 |
| [0171](ADR-0171-the-hand-built-gss-api-kerberos-initiator-sends-mit-flags-and-reads-rfc-4121-and-rfc-4757-tokens-in-strict-sequence.md) | `KerberosGssContext` sends an AP-REQ whose RFC 4121 checksum carries the requested flags plus confidentiality and integrity (as MIT), delegates by `--delegation` level with a caller-supplied forwarded TGT in a session-key KRB-CRED, checks the AP-REP's time echo, and makes and reads RFC 4121 tokens (RFC 4757's for `rc4-hmac`), refusing any acceptor token that is not exactly the next sequence number | Accepted | 2026-09-28 |
| [0172](ADR-0172-http-3-requests-run-on-an-http3session-and-fall-back-to-tcp-only-when-quic-fails.md) | The HTTP handler runs HTTP/3 through `IHttpStreamSession`/`IHttpStreamConnection`, shared with HTTP/2: `Http3Session` (the transfer's `IConnection`, opening curl's control and QPACK streams with the first request) and `Http3StreamConnection` (`HTTP/3 200 ` heads); `--http3-only` on `http://` exit 3, `--http3` tries QUIC then TCP, reporting QUIC's failure when both fail; stream reset exit 95 or 18 after body bytes, stream ended before the head 95, RFC 9114/9204 violations exit 56 with nghttp3's error name; frames capped at 16 MiB | Accepted | 2026-09-28 |
| [0173](ADR-0173-the-hand-built-tls-client-checks-a-stapled-ocsp-response-in-openssls-order-and-fails-the-handshake-with-a-typed-outcome.md) | `OcspStapleVerifier` checks a stapled response in curl's OpenSSL order (parse, `responseStatus`, issuer in the chain, responder ID, signature, issuer or `id-kp-OCSPSigning` delegate, `CertID`, revoked/unknown, then `thisUpdate`/`nextUpdate` with five minutes' leeway against `TimeProvider`) into a typed `OcspStapleOutcome`; with `RequestOcspStatus` both handshakes run it after the verifier accepts the chain and fail with `bad_certificate_status_response` and `CertificateStatusRejection` for BL-610's exit 91; TLS 1.3 builds `status_request` after `supported_groups`; a resumed TLS 1.2 session is not checked | Accepted | 2026-09-28 |
| [0174](ADR-0174-quic-streams-keep-fixed-flow-control-windows-and-one-loop-carries-the-connection.md) | QUIC streams keep fixed flow control windows raised at half (curl turns auto-tuning off), send each BLOCKED frame once per limit, answer STOP_SENDING with RESET_STREAM, and are carried by `QuicConnection`, one loop that owns the datagram channel | Accepted | 2026-09-28 |
| [0175](ADR-0175-the-alt-svc-cache-reads-matches-and-writes-curls-file-as-libcurl-8-21-0-does.md) | `AltSvcCache` in `Curl.Core.UnitLibrary` reads curl's alt-svc file strictly (one space between fields, `h1`/`h2`/`h3`, quoted UTC date, expired entries skipped), reads `Alt-Svc` as 8.21.0 does (`ma` and `persist` per alternative, 24 h default, `clear`, the first known alternative replaces the origin's entries), looks up first match removing expired entries passed, and writes curl's comments and lines with IPv6 brackets and the caller's line ending (CR LF on Windows) | Accepted | 2026-09-28 |
| [0176](ADR-0176-negotiate-is-answered-through-an-asynchronous-security-context-seam-and-sspis-negotiate-never-falls-back-to-ntlm.md) | Measured: with no ticket `--negotiate -u :` sends one request and exits 0 on both platforms. `ISecurityContext.NextTokenAsync` is asynchronous (the hand-built route asks a KDC), `IHttpAuthenticator` gains a default `CreateAuthorizationAsync` the HTTP handler calls; `--negotiate` alone is tried before the first request, after a 401 only with `-u`; on Windows a Negotiate token that falls back to NTLM counts as no credentials, as curl's SSPI gets `SEC_E_NO_CREDENTIALS`; Networking's KDC transport and SRV lookup; `-V` lists `GSS-API`, `Kerberos` and `SPNEGO` | Accepted | 2026-09-28 |
| [0177](ADR-0177-a-quic-connection-closes-times-out-and-resets-as-curls-ngtcp2-build-and-fails-after-the-handshake-with-exit-56-or-55.md) | A QUIC connection sends CONNECTION_CLOSE once, drains after the server's close or a stateless reset, sends a keep-alive PING at half the idle timeout and closes silently at it; after the handshake a lost connection is exit 56 and the idle timeout exit 55 | Accepted | 2026-09-28 |
| [0178](ADR-0178-aws-signature-version-4-signs-as-curls-http-aws-sigv4-c-quirks-included.md) | AWS Signature Version 4 signs as curl's `http_aws_sigv4.c` does: provider, region and service from the parameter or the host, header trimming and merging, the S3 content hash and `UNSIGNED-PAYLOAD`, the S3 path signed as given, the query re-encoded with an empty key written `(nil)`; exits 43, 3, 27 and 100 as measured | Accepted | 2026-09-28 |
| [0179](ADR-0179-the-hsts-cache-reads-matches-and-writes-curls-file-as-libcurl-8-21-0-does.md) | `HstsCache` in `Curl.Core.UnitLibrary` reads curl's HSTS file (`host "date"`, one space, date by `CurlDateParser` or `unlimited`, expired and dotted-subdomain-under-parent lines skipped, duplicates merged), reads `Strict-Transport-Security` as `Curl_hsts_parse` (IP hosts ignored, `max-age=0` removes, updates in place), finds the host's entry or the longest `includeSubDomains` parent, and writes curl's file with the caller's line ending, or nothing past the platform's `gmtime` limit (3001-01-01 20:59:59 UTC on Windows) | Accepted | 2026-09-28 |
| [0180](ADR-0180-tcpconnector-resolves-for-quic-and-quicdialer-tries-each-address-in-turn.md) | `TcpConnector.ConnectMultiplexedAsync` resolves through the TCP path's DNS cache and hands the addresses to `QuicDialer`, which opens a UDP channel through `IUdpChannelOpener` and runs the QUIC handshake at each in turn; a timeout or unusable `--cacert` ends the connect, a socket error is exit 56 `QUIC: recvfrom() …`, and curl's `QUIC connect to` and `Failed to connect to … port` lines follow each failure | Accepted | 2026-09-28 |
| [0181](ADR-0181-http-ntlm-answers-in-three-legs-from-a-fresh-context-per-leg.md) | Measured on both platforms: `--ntlm -u u:p` sends Type 1 up front and Type 3 for the Type 2 on the same connection; a bare `NTLM` gets Type 1 once more, anything after Type 3 ends on the 401, and an unreadable Type 2 is exit 94 on Windows (SSPI) and the 401 elsewhere. `IHttpAuthenticator` gains a default `ContinueAuthorizationAsync` for a 401 to a request that sent a credential, and `HttpAuthenticationFailedException`; each leg comes from a fresh context stepped through Type 1; `-V` lists `NTLM` | Accepted | 2026-09-28 |
| [0182](ADR-0182-http3-options-parse-as-version-options-and-the-console-composes-a-quic-dialer.md) | `--http3` and `--http3-only` are version options (last wins, curl's overrides warning, measured); TCP under either offers `h2,http/1.1`; the console builds one `QuicDialer` from the origin's TLS options for `TcpConnector`, and `EndPointRecordingConnector` forwards QUIC connects | Accepted | 2026-09-29 |
| [0183](ADR-0183-sasl-exchanges-are-awaited-and-security-contexts-wrap-and-unwrap.md) | SASL exchanges are awaited (`GetInitialResponseAsync`, `RespondAsync`), and an established `ISecurityContext` wraps and unwraps messages | Accepted | 2026-09-29 |
| [0184](ADR-0184-sasl-gssapi-and-ntlm-run-on-one-security-context-per-exchange.md) | SASL GSSAPI (raw Kerberos, RFC 4752 security layer: none, size 0, the authzid, wrapped unencrypted) and NTLM (Type 1, then Type 3) run on one security context per exchange for `<service>/<host>`, ranked as curl 8.21.0 ranks them; a failed step still cancels with exit 67 until BL-856 | Accepted | 2026-09-29 |
| [0185](ADR-0185-a-hand-built-zstandard-decoder-lives-in-curl-zstandard-unitlibrary-and-references-nothing.md) | A hand-built Zstandard (RFC 8878) decoder and XXH64 live in `Curl.Zstandard.UnitLibrary`, which references nothing; a `BrotliDecoder`-shaped push `ZstandardDecoder` (window limit 2^27 by default, as libzstd) plus one-shot `TryDecompress`, used by HTTP `--compressed` and RFC 8879 in `Curl.Tls`; amends ADR-0120 | Accepted | 2026-09-29 |
| [0186](ADR-0186-a-connect-tunnel-answers-a-407-through-the-injected-proxy-authenticator.md) | A CONNECT tunnel answers a 407 through the injected proxy authenticator, with the scheme `--proxy-digest` or `--proxy-anyauth` chose, instead of always sending Basic | Accepted | 2026-09-29 |
| [0187](ADR-0187-http-3-stream-resets-follow-curl-8-21-0-and-a-refused-stream-is-retried-on-a-new-connection.md) | HTTP/3 follows `curl-8_21_0`, the release Curl reports: a reset prints `HTTP/3 stream <id> reset by server (error 0x<hex> <name>)` (95, or 18 after body bytes); `H3_REQUEST_REJECTED` prints `… refused by server, try again on a new connection` and is retried on a new connection up to 5 times, independent of `--retry`, then fails 56 `Connection died, tried 5 times before giving up`; `-I` ignores a reset after the head; lists the other rows that move from 8.18.0 | Accepted | 2026-09-29 |
| [0188](ADR-0188-service-names-and-delegation-reach-every-security-context-and-sspi-never-delegates.md) | Negotiate asks for `--proxy-service-name` (proxy) or `--service-name` (server), else `HTTP`, with the `--delegation` level; SASL takes `--service-name`; SSPI never delegates, as the Schannel build never reads the option; the system GSS-API delegates for `always` only, since `NegotiateAuthentication` cannot ask `GSS_C_DELEG_POLICY_FLAG` | Accepted | 2026-09-29 |
| [0189](ADR-0189-proto-scheme-sets-use-one-known-scheme-list-on-every-platform.md) | `--proto`, `--proto-redir` and `--proto-default` know one scheme list on every platform (the Windows Schannel curl 8.21.0 `Protocols:` line less `ipfs` and `ipns`, plus `smb` and `smbs` since BL-1099); sets are lowercase and unordered, `null` when not given; an unknown `--proto-default`, `all` included, is refused with exit 1 | Accepted | 2026-09-29 |
| [0190](ADR-0190-an-https-proxy-handshake-offers-http-1-1-through-alpn.md) | The TLS handshake with an HTTPS proxy, tunnel or forward, offers `http/1.1` alone through ALPN whatever the HTTP version options say, and nothing under `--no-alpn` (measured on Schannel and OpenSSL); carrying `--no-alpn` to the proxy's options is a follow-up | Accepted | 2026-09-29 |
| [0191](ADR-0191-cert-status-runs-the-hand-built-client-and-ssl-auto-client-cert-reads-the-personal-store-everywhere.md) | `--cert-status` routes to the hand-built TLS client on every platform and fails a missing, revoked or invalid stapled OCSP response with exit 91 and OpenSSL's `verifystatus()` text (the Schannel build's silence is not matched); `--ssl-auto-client-cert` (and its proxy form) presents the first `CurrentUser\MY` certificate with its key, valid now and allowed for client authentication, on every platform; no option produces exit 83 | Accepted | 2026-09-29 |
| [0192](ADR-0192-a-zstandard-compressed-block-of-size-0-is-an-empty-block.md) | A Zstandard compressed block of size 0 decodes as an empty block, as curl's streaming libzstd does; four Huffman streams of fewer than 6 literals are `LiteralsHeaderWrong`, other malformed literals `CorruptionDetected`; blocks with sequences are `SequencesNotYetSupported` until BL-860 | Accepted | 2026-09-29 |
| [0193](ADR-0193-pinnedpubkey-is-checked-in-the-shared-certificate-judgement-on-every-platform.md) | `--pinnedpubkey` (`sha256//` hashes or a PEM or DER key file) is checked in `ServerCertificateVerification.Judge` after verification, under `-k` too, in both TLS providers on every platform; a mismatch, or a missing or keyless file, is exit 90 `SSL: public key does not match pinned public key` (measured on Schannel and OpenSSL); the `-v` lines are BL-877's | Accepted | 2026-09-29 |
| [0194](ADR-0194-the-hand-built-kerberos-reads-dir-and-kcm-credential-caches-as-mit-does.md) | The hand-built Kerberos reads `DIR:` collections (MIT's `primary` rules, a `/` refused) and `KCM:` caches (MIT's `cc_kcm.c` opcodes over `kcm_socket`, `-` turns it off), falls back to `default_ccache_name` with `%{uid}`/`%{euid}`, and reports KCM failures as typed errors | Accepted | 2026-09-29 |
| [0195](ADR-0195-tls-1-0-and-1-1-rsa-client-signatures-use-a-hand-built-blinded-crt-private-key-operation.md) | A TLS 1.0 and 1.1 RSA CertificateVerify is signed by the hand-built `RsaCrtPrivateKey` - RSASP1 on the CRT values, blinded with r^-1 by Fermat, checked against e before release; a non-exportable key still sends an empty Certificate | Accepted | 2026-09-29 |
| [0196](ADR-0196-zstandard-matches-reach-back-as-far-as-libzstd-buffers.md) | A Zstandard match may reach back as far as libzstd's streaming decoder buffers (`Window_Size` plus two `Block_Maximum_Size` plus 64 bytes), not only `Window_Size`; every malformed sequences section is `CorruptionDetected`, and `SequencesNotYetSupported` is gone | Accepted | 2026-09-29 |
| [0197](ADR-0197-crlfile-is-checked-by-hand-in-the-openssl-build-and-ignored-by-the-schannel-build.md) | `--crlfile` is ignored by the Schannel build, as curl.exe ignores it; the OpenSSL build, unless `-k`, loads its PEM lists (exit 82 `error loading CRL file: <path>` when it cannot) and checks every certificate of the verified chain against a list from its issuer, decoded and signature-checked by hand, failing with exit 60 and OpenSSL's verify result (`certificate revoked (23)`, `unable to get certificate CRL (3)`, `CRL signature failure (8)`, measured) | Accepted | 2026-09-29 |
| [0198](ADR-0198-quic-cubic-runs-hystart-plus-plus-and-holds-the-window-while-application-limited.md) | QUIC CUBIC runs HyStart++ (RFC 9406) with ngtcp2's constants in the first slow start, rounds by send time and one RTT sample per acknowledgement; a flush that leaves window unused marks its packets application-limited, and neither CUBIC nor NewReno grows the window for them (RFC 9002 section 7.8), CUBIC also stopping its epoch clock | Accepted | 2026-09-29 |
| [0199](ADR-0199-the-hand-built-tls-1-3-client-decompresses-server-certificates-to-rfc-8879.md) | The hand-built TLS 1.3 client offers `compress_certificate` from `CertificateCompressionAlgorithms` (the OpenSSL profile keeps the captured zlib and zstd) and decompresses a CompressedCertificate with zlib, brotli or zstd; a wrong length, an algorithm not offered or corrupt data is `bad_certificate` | Accepted | 2026-09-29 |
| [0200](ADR-0200-smb-and-smbs-speak-curls-smbv1-nt-lm-0-12-on-every-platform.md) | `smb` and `smbs` are offered on every platform and speak curl's SMBv1 (one dialect, `NT LM 0.12`, measured) with NTLMv1 LM and NT responses from `Curl.Ntlm.UnitLibrary`; every platform matches the OpenSSL build's text, and tests drive the handler through `IConnection` from recorded bytes | Accepted | 2026-09-29 |
